| level-c-nonfiction-01-bees | Bees | 3 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Worker bees fly from flower to flower. They collect a sweet liquid from inside flowers. This liquid is called nectar. | approved |
| level-c-nonfiction-01-bees | Bees | 4 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Bees carry nectar back to the hive. They pass it from bee to bee. Slowly, it turns into honey. | approved |
| level-c-nonfiction-01-bees | Bees | 5 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Bees also collect yellow dust called pollen. Pollen sticks to their fuzzy legs. When the bee visits the next flower, some pollen falls off. This helps plants make seeds. | approved |
| level-c-nonfiction-01-bees | Bees | 6 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | This is called pollination. Without bees, many plants could not make seeds. No seeds means no fruits or vegetables. We need bees very much. | approved |
| level-c-nonfiction-01-bees | Bees | 7 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | One jar of honey needs thousands of bee trips to make. | level-c-nonfiction-01-bees | Bees | 8 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata; text noun may conflict with image metadata | We can help bees by planting flowers. | level-c-nonfiction-01-bees | Bees | 9 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Bees work hard every day. They help flowers grow. They make our world sweeter. Bees are amazing. | approved |
| level-c-nonfiction-02-volcanoes | Volcanoes | 1 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata; text noun may conflict with image metadata | Deep underground, rock gets so hot that it melts. | level-c-nonfiction-02-volcanoes | Volcanoes | 2 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Sometimes, magma pushes up through a crack in the Earth. When it bursts out at the surface, we call it a volcano! The melted rock that flows out is called lava. | approved |

| level-c-nonfiction-02-volcanoes | Volcanoes | 4 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata; text noun may conflict with image metadata | Other volcanoes are very explosive. | level-c-nonfiction-02-volcanoes | Volcanoes | 5 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata; text noun may conflict with image metadata | Volcanoes can be found on land and under the sea. | level-c-nonfiction-02-volcanoes | Volcanoes | 6 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | After a volcano, the cooled lava becomes new rock. | level-c-nonfiction-02-volcanoes | Volcanoes | 8 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Most volcanoes are quiet for a very long time. | level-c-nonfiction-03-penguins | Penguins | 1 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Penguins are birds — but they cannot fly. Instead, they use their wings as flippers. They are brilliant swimmers. | approved |

| level-c-nonfiction-03-penguins | Penguins | 3 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | The female emperor penguin lays one egg. | level-c-nonfiction-03-penguins | Penguins | 4 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | The father does not eat for two months. | level-c-nonfiction-03-penguins | Penguins | 5 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | When the egg hatches, a tiny chick comes out. The chick has thick grey fluffy feathers. It cuddles close to its parent to stay warm. | approved |

| level-c-nonfiction-03-penguins | Penguins | 7 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Penguins live in large groups called colonies. | level-c-nonfiction-03-penguins | Penguins | 8 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Penguins are black on top and white underneath. | level-c-nonfiction-03-penguins | Penguins | 9 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | There are 18 species of penguin. Not all live in cold places. Little blue penguins live in Australia. African penguins live on warm, sunny beaches. Every kind of penguin is remarkable. | approved |
| level-c-nonfiction-04-the-moon | The Moon | 1 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | The Moon is a huge ball of rock. | level-c-nonfiction-04-the-moon | The Moon | 2 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | The Moon does not make its own light. It has no fire or glow inside. The light we see is sunlight bouncing off the Moon's surface. | approved |

| level-c-nonfiction-04-the-moon | The Moon | 4 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | The Moon's surface is covered in craters. | level-c-nonfiction-04-the-moon | The Moon | 5 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | In 1969, astronauts travelled to the Moon. Neil Armstrong was the first human to walk on its surface. "That's one small step for man," he said. | approved |
| level-c-nonfiction-04-the-moon | The Moon | 6 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | The Moon's gravity pulls on Earth's oceans. | level-c-nonfiction-04-the-moon | The Moon | 7 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | The astronauts brought Moon rocks back to Earth. | level-c-nonfiction-04-the-moon | The Moon | 8 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | The Moon looks small from Earth, but it is very large. | level-c-nonfiction-05-how-seeds-grow | How Seeds Grow | 1 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Almost every plant starts as a tiny seed. | level-c-nonfiction-05-how-seeds-grow | How Seeds Grow | 2 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | A seed needs three things to start growing: water, warmth, and air. When it gets all three, it wakes up and begins to grow. | approved |

| level-c-nonfiction-06-spiders | Spiders | 4 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Not all webs look the same. Sheet webs are flat like a hammock. Cobwebs are messy tangles. Every type of spider makes its own kind of web. | approved |

| level-c-nonfiction-06-spiders | Spiders | 8 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Female spiders lay eggs in a silk sac. The sac protects the eggs. When they hatch, tiny spiderlings emerge. Some mothers carry the sac everywhere. | approved |

| level-c-nonfiction-07-under-the-ocean | Under the Ocean | 1 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | The ocean covers more than two thirds of our planet. It is the largest habitat on Earth. And most of it is still unexplored. | approved |
| level-c-nonfiction-07-under-the-ocean | Under the Ocean | 2 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | The ocean has different layers. | level-c-nonfiction-07-under-the-ocean | Under the Ocean | 3 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | The top layer is full of life. | level-c-nonfiction-07-under-the-ocean | Under the Ocean | 4 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Coral looks like rock or plant, but it is an animal. | level-c-nonfiction-07-under-the-ocean | Under the Ocean | 5 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Further down, in the deep and dark, strange creatures live. | level-c-nonfiction-07-under-the-ocean | Under the Ocean | 6 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | In the deepest, darkest water, some creatures make their own light. | level-c-nonfiction-07-under-the-ocean | Under the Ocean | 9 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | More than 80% of the ocean has never been explored. | level-c-nonfiction-08-butterflies | Butterflies | 1 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Butterflies are insects. Like all insects, they have six legs, four wings, and a body in three parts. They also have two long antennae. | approved |
| level-c-nonfiction-08-butterflies | Butterflies | 2 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | A butterfly's life has four stages. This is called a lifecycle. Each stage looks completely different from the one before. | approved |
| level-c-nonfiction-08-butterflies | Butterflies | 3 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Stage one: the butterfly lays tiny eggs on a leaf. The eggs are very small — some are no bigger than a pinhead. The leaf is food for the caterpillar inside. | approved |
| level-c-nonfiction-08-butterflies | Butterflies | 4 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Stage two: the egg hatches into a caterpillar. The caterpillar's only job is to eat. It eats and eats and grows and grows. Some caterpillars eat their own eggshell first! | approved |
| level-c-nonfiction-08-butterflies | Butterflies | 5 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Stage three: the caterpillar makes a hard shell around itself. This is called a chrysalis. Inside, an extraordinary change is about to happen. | approved |
| level-c-nonfiction-08-butterflies | Butterflies | 6 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Inside the chrysalis, the caterpillar breaks down completely. | level-c-nonfiction-08-butterflies | Butterflies | 7 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Stage four: the butterfly pushes out of the chrysalis. Its wings are wet and crumpled. The butterfly hangs still and waits. Slowly, its wings unfurl and dry. | approved |
| level-c-nonfiction-08-butterflies | Butterflies | 8 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Butterflies drink nectar from flowers. | level-c-nonfiction-08-butterflies | Butterflies | 9 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Some butterflies migrate thousands of kilometres. | level-c-nonfiction-09-caves | Caves | 1 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Caves are hollow spaces underground or inside cliffs. | level-c-nonfiction-09-caves | Caves | 2 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Most caves form when rainwater seeps into limestone rock. | level-c-nonfiction-09-caves | Caves | 3 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Over time, tunnels and chambers form underground. | level-c-nonfiction-09-caves | Caves | 4 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Water drips from cave ceilings for thousands of years. | level-c-nonfiction-09-caves | Caves | 5 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | When drips hit the cave floor, minerals build up there too. | level-c-nonfiction-09-caves | Caves | 8 | missing imageAlt metadata; missing pageDescription metadata; missing targetWords metadata | Some creatures live only in caves and never come out. ## Requirements For Replacement Pack

- Page images must be illustration-only, with no embedded story text unless the app text exactly matches it.
- Page narration must match the app text word-for-word.
- Each page needs imageAlt, pageDescription, embeddedImageText, targetWords, decodableFocus, highFrequencyWords, qaStatus, and qaNotes.
- Level A/B sentences can stay simple, but they must be coherent, natural, capitalized, and punctuated.
- Do not reuse the current mismatched page art as active reader content.
